      "full_text": "After rejecting a previous offer, payments giant PayPal is considering a new deal to sell itself to a group of investors that includes private financial company Stripe, according to a Wall Street Journal report Friday.\nIn July, Stripe and private equity firm Advent International made a joint offer to buy the digital payments pioneer for $60.50 a share, which would have valued PayPal at around $53 billion, according to media reports.\nThat's an 85% haircut compared to PayPal's peak market value which was near $360 billion in 2021. The company issued disappointing profit guidance for 2026 earlier this year.\nPayPal has struggled to compete with other payments providers including Apple Pay and Google Pay.\nThe parties have continued negotiating since the previous deal fell through, and a new agreement could materialize \"in the coming weeks,\" according to the WSJ.\nIt's unclear how much a new deal might value PayPal, but the company's shares jumped after the news and closed up nearly 1.8%.\nPayPal declined to comment.\nBrothers Patrick and John Collison founded Stripe in 2010 to facilitate online payments for businesses.\nStripe is still a privately held company but is considered a strong contender for an eventual IPO. It's market value is close to $185 billion, according to Forge Global, a company that tracks the share prices of private companies.\nPayPal was co-founded in 1998 by billionaire investor Peter Thiel, and Elon Musk became an early partner after the company merged with his startup X.com.\nThe company pioneered allowing people to send each other money via online payments, known as peer-to-peer payments.\nIts early executives became known in Silicon Valley as the \"PayPal mafia,\" a group of alumni who went on to found or lead companies including Tesla, SpaceX, LinkedIn, YouTube and Palantir.\nIn July, Stripe and private equity firm Advent International made a joint offer to buy the digital payments pioneer for $60.50 a share, which would have valued PayPal at around $53 billion, according to media reports.\nThat's an 85% haircut compared to PayPal's peak market value which was near $360 billion in 2021. The company issued disappointing profit guidance for 2026 earlier this year.\nPayPal has struggled to compete with other payments providers including Apple Pay and Google Pay.\nThe parties have continued negotiating since the previous deal fell through, and a new agreement could materialize \"in the coming weeks,\" according to the WSJ.\nIt's unclear how much a new deal might value PayPal, but the company's shares jumped after the news and closed up nearly 1.8%.\nPayPal declined to comment.\nBrothers Patrick and John Collison founded Stripe in 2010 to facilitate online payments for businesses.\nStripe is still a privately held company but is considered a strong contender for an eventual IPO. It's market value is close to $185 billion, according to Forge Global, a company that tracks the share prices of private companies.\nPayPal was co-founded in 1998 by billionaire investor Peter Thiel, and Elon Musk became an early partner after the company merged with his startup X.com.\nThe company pioneered allowing people to send each other money via online payments, known as peer-to-peer payments.\nIts early executives became known in Silicon Valley as the \"PayPal mafia,\" a group of alumni who went on to found or lead companies including Tesla, SpaceX, LinkedIn, YouTube and Palantir."

Privacy Policy\n\nWhile some incumbents in the traditional financial sector have been slow to adopt fintech innovation, over the past few years, fintech adoption has... Becoming a Fintech Professional: 5 Steps to Success! Posted on August 14, 2026 on Graduate School, Seahawk Nation Pinterest Email Share While some incumbents in the traditional financial sector have been slow to adopt fintech innovation, over the past few years, fintech adoption has accelerated as people search for faster, safer, and more efficient ways to conduct their finances. Fintech can make it easier for people to access financial services and products and help businesses manage their finances more efficiently. If you’re interested in learning more about fintech as a profession, this article is for you! Start or Advance Your FinTech Career with Keiser Whether you are beginning a career in financial technology or seeking to advance your existing career, Keiser University’s Master of Science in Financial Technology can help you develop the technical, analytical, and strategic knowledge needed to navigate an evolving financial services landscape. Through graduate-level study and applied learning, students can explore areas such as financial technologies, data analytics, artificial intelligence, digital financial services, and emerging technologies while developing skills applicable to real-world business challenges. Keiser University’s experienced faculty bring academic and professional expertise into the learning environment, helping students connect theory with practical applications and prepare for opportunities in the evolving FinTech industry. Take the next step toward becoming a FinTech leader with Keiser University’s Master of Science in Financial Technology. Connect with our team today to learn more about how the program can help you reach your career goals. What Is a Fintech Professional? Fintech professionals work at the intersection of finance, technology, data, and innovation. They apply technological and analytical solutions to improve financial products, services, operations, and customer experiences. Depending on their role, fintech professionals may work with artificial intelligence and machine learning, data analytics, digital payments, blockchain technologies, cybersecurity, fraud detection, regulatory technology (RegTech), or other emerging financial technologies. Fintech professionals work across a variety of organizations, including banks, financial institutions, technology companies, consulting firms, startups, insurance companies, and other organizations undergoing digital transformation. Their responsibilities may include developing innovative financial products, analyzing financial data, improving digital transactions, managing technology-related risks, detecting and preventing fraud, and identifying new ways technology can make financial services more efficient, secure, and accessible. Fintech professionals come from diverse educational and professional backgrounds, including finance, technology, business, data analytics, cybersecurity, and related disciplines. Successful professionals in this field often combine technical knowledge with analytical thinking, problem-solving, adaptability, and an understanding of financial systems. As financial services continue to become increasingly digital and technologies such as artificial intelligence, blockchain, data analytics, and automation reshape the industry, professionals with interdisciplinary fintech knowledge can contribute to innovation across a wide range of organizations and roles. Ethics & Cybersecurity in FinTech Trust is fundamental to the financial technology ecosystem. Consumers and institutions depend on fintech professionals and organizations to protect sensitive financial and personal data, promote fairness in technology-enabled decision-making, and maintain transparency across digital financial systems. As innovation accelerates and technologies such as artificial intelligence become increasingly integrated into financial services, ethical considerations, cybersecurity, data privacy, responsible AI, regulatory compliance, and effective risk management become increasingly important. Fintech professionals should prioritize data privacy, responsible data governance, and ethical considerations throughout the design and implementation of financial technologies. For AI-enabled systems, this includes evaluating models for potential bias, monitoring performance and model drift, and assessing whether automated outcomes are fair, explainable, and appropriate for their intended use. Strong cybersecurity practices are equally important. Measures such as encryption, identity and access management, multifactor authentication, least-privilege access, secure system design, and continuous monitoring can help protect sensitive financial and personal information. Fintech organizations must also consider regulatory and financial-crime compliance requirements, including: Know Your Customer (KYC) and Anti-Money Laundering (AML) protocols, encryption, and least-privilege access. These practices help protect users and maintain compliance with global financial regulations. Transparency and user-centered design further strengthen trust. Financial technologies should communicate clearly how data is collected and used and, where appropriate, how technology-assisted decisions affect consumers. Together, responsible governance, cybersecurity, regulatory compliance, and transparency can support secure and ethical financial innovation. How to Become a Fintech Professional As financial services become increasingly digital, fintech offers career opportunities for professionals from a variety of backgrounds—not only those already working in finance. Individuals with experience or education in technology, data analytics, cybersecurity, business, finance, and related disciplines can bring valuable skills to the field. Understanding the knowledge, competencies, and career pathways associated with fintech can help prospective professionals determine how best to prepare for opportunities in this evolving industry. We met with Theo Owusu, D.Sc., and Dr. Donald DeSormoux, Dean – Graduate School at Keiser University. We asked them to help us better understand how a person interested in becoming a fintech professional might go about it. 1. Figure Out if It’s the Right Career for You One way to learn more about fintech is to attend industry events or meetups. These provide an excellent opportunity to network with others in the field and get a feel for the kind of work that goes on in fintech. You can also read up on the latest industry news and trends to help you understand the challenges and opportunities that fintech companies face. And if you’re still unsure, why not reach out to someone who already works in fintech? They’ll be able to give you an insider’s view of what it’s like to work in this exciting industry. Good Qualities for a Fintech Pro As the financial sector evolves, so do the skillsets require its professionals. With the rise of fintech, those working in finance must be comfortable with innovative technologies and able to adapt to change. Here are some qualities and soft skills that all fintech professionals should have: Critical Thinking/Problem Solving: Strong critical thinking skills are essential for any financial technology professional. A fintech professional should be able to think both creatively and critically to provide innovative solutions to complex problems. Patience: O ne of the most essential qualities to have is patience. Fintech pros know that it takes time to understand technologies and trends. You also need to be patient when working with clients and customers as they may not be familiar with the latest technologies . Data Analysis: To succeed in fintech, you need to understand and interpret data, identify trends and patterns, and use that information to develop solutions. Strong Interest in Finance & Technology: While there are many different fintech roles, they all require a solid foundation in both finance and technology. An interest in and aptitude for both is, therefore, essential. Curiosity: To succeed in fintech, you need to adapt to change and embrace innovative ideas . A curious mind will help you stay ahead of the curve and identify new opportunities. In addition, being curious will help you build a network of contacts and resources. Communication: Effective communicators in the fintech industry can distill complex topics down to their essence and communicate clearly and concisely, both in writing and in person. Professionals also require strong listening skills to understand client needs. Adaptability: Finally, a fintech professional should be adaptable. As a result, fintech professionals need to be able to keep up with the latest trends and developments and learn new skills quickly. 2. Earn a Degree in Fintech Related Field If you have the qualities of a successful fintech professional, the next step is to pursue formal education in the field. While there are many ways to learn about financial technology, a comprehensive understanding of the subject matter can be gained through formal study. Fortunately, there are now many options for pursuing such an education. Online courses, evening classes, and flexible programs are increasingly common, making it easier than ever for you to gain the theoretical and practical skills that are essential for success in this rapidly growing industry. Undergraduate Degrees: An undergraduate degree in information technology, computer science, or engineering will give you the technical skills to start your formal education in the field of fintech. A business, economics, or finance degree will provide you with the financial knowledge you need. No matter what route you choose, make sure you select a program that is accredited by a reputable organization.\n\nMaster’s Degree: Dr. Owusu stresses that a quality master’s program will emphasize hands-on experiences that will help the development of practical skills. Graduate-level programs in financial technology, such as the one offered at Keiser University , cover topics vital to professionals in this field, including data analysis, cryptocurrency and blockchain technology , financial modeling, and more.\n\nCertification: Various fintech certification opportunities exist, but according to Dr. Owusu, certification on its own can quickly become outdated. Certificates tend to only last for up to five years, at which point you would need to reapply for certification. 3. Learn Common Software Used in Financial Technology When working in the financial technology sector, there are specific software programs that professionals commonly use. These are just a few of the most widely used software programs in the fintech world that you should prepare to familiarize yourself with: Tableau: Tableau is a data visualization software program that allows users to create graphs, charts, and maps from data sets. Businesses and organizations of all sizes use it to help them make better decisions by understanding their data more clearly. Fintech professionals can use Tableau to visualize data from various sources, including Excel files, SQL databases, and web data.\n\nPower BI: Power BI is a software product created by Microsoft that transforms data from various sources into interactive insights. With Power BI, you can input data directly from databases, webpages, and structured files such as spreadsheets and JSON files.\n\nExcel: Excel is a program where you can type in data and make graphs and charts from it. It is one of the most commonly used software programs in the business world and is an essential tool for any fintech professional.\n\nR: R is a programming language and environment for statistical computing. R has many statistical and graphical methods that are highly extensible. The ability to generate well-designed publication-quality plots, including mathematical symbols and formulae where needed, is one of R’s strengths .\n\nPython: Python is a language fintech professional used for multiple purposes. Its design focuses on code readability through significant indentation of use. Python also does not require the user to define variables and collect data automatically after execution. It supports different paradigms, such as object-oriented programming, functional programming, and structured programming.\n\nSplunk: Splunk software is a machine data analytics platform that enables users to search, monitor, and analyze data from any source. The platform can be used to investigate a wide variety of data, including system logs, application logs, network traffic, and more. Splunk software is designed to be easy to use and can be deployed in a matter of minutes. 4. Join the FinTech Community The competition in the financial technology industry is fierce. It’s vital to join the fintech community to succeed as a professional in this field. Many of the leading figures in fintech are active community members, so you’ll have a chance to learn from the best and brightest minds in the field. By connecting with other fintech professionals, you can stay up to date on the latest industry trends, find new business opportunities, and build valuable relationships. There are many ways to get involved in the fintech community. One of the best ways is to attend fintech events. These events bring together professionals from all over the world to discuss the latest industry trends and innovations. Some of these events are free to attend, so there’s no excuse not to get involved. Another great way to get involved in the fintech community is to join an online forum or discussion group. These groups provide a great way to connect with other professionals, share ideas, and stay up to date on the latest industry news. Several professional organizations cater to fintech professionals, such as the Financial Technology Association (FTA) and the Fintech Innovation Lab. These organizations offer many benefits, including networking opportunities, educational resources, and access to industry news and research. There are many influential fintech leaders you can follow on social media. This is a great way to stay up to date on industry news and insights. Twitter is a particularly useful platform for following fintech leaders, as many of them are active users of the site. Finally, you can also get involved by contributing to fintech publications. There are a number of online publications that accept submissions from industry experts. By writing for these publications, you can share your insights with the fintech community and build your reputation as a thought leader. 5. Find a Job & Continue Growing Now that you have the skills and experience necessary to enter the fintech field, it’s time to start searching for a job. The first step is to identify the type of position you’re interested in. Do you want to work in a bank, an insurance company, or a fintech startup? Once you’ve decided on the type of employer you’re looking for, you can begin searching for specific job openings. You can search for fintech positions on online job boards, but another option is to reach out to your professional network. If you know someone who works in fintech, they may be able to put you in touch with a hiring manager or help you land an interview. If you land a job in the burgeoning financial technology field, congratulations! You’re currently riding the wave of one of the hottest industries around. But don’t get too comfortable – as with any rapidly evolving field, the landscape of fintech can change quickly, and it’s crucial to stay ahead of the curve if you want to keep your job. That means continuously learning and growing your skillset. Whether taking an online course or attending industry events, it’s crucial to ensure you’re always up-to-date on the latest developments. By committing to lifelong learning, you’ll ensure that you always have a place in the ever-changing world of financial technology. How Keiser University Prepares FinTech Leaders Keiser University’s Master of Science in Financial Technology is built for working professionals who want to advance their expertise through applied, hands-on learning. The program blends core financial theory with emerging technologies that are reshaping the global financial landscape, providing the following: Core domains: Students develop proficiency across data analytics, artificial intelligence and machine learning (AI/ML) for finance, blockchain and cryptocurrency, cybersecurity, and financial modeling.\n\nPractice-first learning: Through labs, projects, and close collaboration with experienced faculty, learners gain the opportunity to apply what they learn in real-world scenarios, bridging the gap between classroom concepts and industry application.\n\nCareer-ready outcomes: Graduates leave the program with a professional portfolio that showcases their analytical models, interactive dashboards, and fintech product prototypes — demonstrating the technical and strategic skills employers value most. With a curriculum rooted in practical application and ethical responsibility, Keiser University equips professionals to navigate the fast-changing financial technology landscape with confidence and purpose. Frequently Asked Questions About Financial Technology Do I need a finance or CS background? While a background in finance or computer science can be helpful, it isn’t always required. Many fintech professionals come from diverse fields such as business, engineering, or data analytics. What matters most is your willingness to learn and your comfort working with both financial concepts and digital tools. Which programming languages should I learn first? Some of the most commonly used programming languages in fintech include Python, R, SQL, and Java. Python is often recommended for beginners because it’s versatile and widely used in data analytics, machine learning, and financial modeling. As you advance, learning specialized tools like Tableau or Power BI can help you visualize data and communicate insights effectively. How fast can I pivot into FinTech? The answer depends on your experience and how you approach your learning path. Professionals with a strong base in finance or technology may transition within months by upskilling through focused coursework or certifications. Those starting from a different field might take longer but can still build momentum quickly through structured programs, practical projects, and networking within the fintech community. How do I get started? Begin by developing your understanding of both financial systems and emerging technologies. Explore online resources, attend fintech meetups, or enroll in a formal program that blends business and tech education. Look for opportunities to gain hands-on experience — such as internships, research projects, or data-driven case studies — that can strengthen your professional portfolio. Preparing for Life After Your Degree Graduating with an MBA or advanced degree in financial technology marks the start of a new professional chapter. To make the most of your education, focus on applying what you’ve learned to real-world challenges and setting clear career goals. Stay engaged with your professional network, explore emerging fintech trends, and continue building skills through ongoing education or certifications. The most successful fintech professionals are lifelong learners who adapt quickly to new technologies and industry demands. Take the next step toward becoming a fintech leader with Keiser University’s Master of Science in Financial Technology. Connect with our team today to learn more about how this program can help you reach your career goals.       "full_text": "Intuit Expands AI-Powered Intelligence into the Mid-Market By Daisy Hawker Share this article Prioritise Us on Google Share this article Prioritise Us on Google Intuit is making changes to their flagship services. Credit: Getty Images A fintech SaaS is bringing enterprise-grade AI to the mid-market, aiming to stop growing companies from abandoning QuickBooks for costly ERP migrations Mid-market finance teams are undergoing a massive technological shift. To support scaling organisations navigating multi-entity structures and complex compliance, Intuit announced a major expansion of its flagship system, Intuit Intelligence, across its mid-market portfolio. The expansion will bring new capabilities to QuickBooks Online Advanced and its AI-native Enterprise Resource Planning (ERP), Intuit Enterprise Suite. This is set to provide finance teams with instant, auditable and actionable clarity across their core accounting and compliance workflows. Moving AI into high-control finance workflows The adoption of AI-driven financial platforms is accelerating across mid-market leadership. According to Intuit’s 2026 Future of Finance Report, leaders at high-growth businesses are more than twice as likely to use AI in financial workflows compared to slower-growth peers. However, as automation handles cash management and forecasting, proper governance remains critical. Ashley Still believes teams need AI they can stand behind when asked how a number was reached “Finance teams need AI they can stand behind when asked how a number was reached,” says Ashley Still, Executive Vice President and General Manager of Small Business and Mid-Market for Intuit. “Our latest innovations give finance leaders the industry-specific depth and instant insights they've been asking for, built into the way they already work.” Ray Wang, Principal Analyst and Founder at Constellation Research, adds: “Customers seek AI-native ERP solutions with a serious depth of expertise. Ray Wang provides insights from Constellation Research. Credit: Constellation Research “Clients expect their new ERP offerings to have rebuilt the core financial process, close, reconciliation and forecasting, around AI as the default way work gets done. “That’s a meaningfully different bet, and it’s the kind of shift that separates vendors who talk about AI from vendors who are actually restructuring their platform around it.” One of the developments for this vision is Intuit Intelligence Chat, a conversational AI tool enabling CFOs to analyse multi-entity performance and trigger workflows in plain language. The system will act only after user confirmation, maintaining strict auditability. Consolidating fragmented tools with QuickBooks Online Advanced To prevent up-and-coming companies from relying on a patchwork of tools, QuickBooks Online Advanced folds bill pay, payments, business intelligence and continuous book keeping directly into one core subscription. Bill Pay Elite adds a full accounts payable hub with free Automated Clearing House (ACH) and 1099 e-filing at no extra subscription cost. Additionally, Books Upkeep now uses AI to categorise transactions continuously, only surfacing exceptions needing human approval. Intuit believes the impact is already largely positive.\n\nAccording to its research, 83% of QuickBooks Online Advanced customers say the platform gives them the data they need to make better decisions. AI-driven workflows and ledger processing Rather than relying on periodic batch processing like traditional software, Intuit’s platform leverages machine learning models trained on historical entries to continuously process data across industry-specific workflows. In construction, predictive AI algorithms analyse historical performance and live job data to generate estimates and detect potential budget overruns before they impact margins. For manufacturing and larger non-profit financial operations, automated dimensional tagging applies intelligent rules to multi-level bills of materials and fund allocations, eliminating the manual conversion maths which can introduce errors into cost of goods sold and restricted fund tracking. At the enterprise layer, AI extends directly into inter-company accounting.\n\nIntuit Enterprise Suite utilises pattern-recognition models to auto-draft inter-company account mappings and recurring transaction templates, allowing the month-end close to execute in the background as a continuous process. When migrating legacy financial data, autonomous readiness agents will pre-validate historical records, including inventory costing and multi-currency exchange parameters adhering to ASC 830 and IAS 21.\n\nIntuit's research notes this will enable 95% of platforms to go live in 30 days or less. Company portals Intuit Executives Ashley Still Executive Vice President, General Manager, Mid-Market"

      "group_id": 6879369,
      "full_text": "Most mid-market CFOs are not going to build OpenAI’s finance stack. They do not have the same engineering resources, access to frontier models or appetite for rebuilding finance workflows from first principles.\n\nThey have an ERP, a planning system, Excel, a lean team and a month-end close that still depends on people knowing where the bodies are buried. But on Monday (Aug. 10), Open AI CFO Sarah Friar published a vision of an “AI-native finance function” that is built atop a “zero day close.” The relevant idea for non-AI-native firms and finance teams is not the zero-day close itself. It is what that goal reveals about where finance automation is heading: away from periodic reporting and toward continuously reconciled, decision-ready information. For a mid-market CFO, that means the more useful question is not necessarily, “Can artificial intelligence help us close the books faster?” It is, “Which decisions are currently being made with stale financial information?” The Real AI KPI Is Decision Latency New research from PYMNTS Intelligence’s “The Enterprise AI Benchmark Report” reveals that more than 7 in 10 executives (71%) at companies with $1 billion or more in annual revenue believe that organizational readiness is the primary limitation on AI performance. Meanwhile, just 11% think that AI technology itself is the main barrier. “We see inconsistent and incomplete data structures, bad data, dirty data,” Michael Younkie, VP of Product Management at Billtrust, told PYMNTS. “We see challenges around legacy ERP systems with limited AR API capabilities.” Friar argued that CFOs should determine which data AI can access, which actions it can take, when approval is required and when an exception must be escalated. Every output should tie back to a reliable source, and changes to approved forecast baselines should remain under finance control. CFOs of AI-native finance functions should measure whether useful work was completed, what it cost after human review and rework, whether the output was usable and whether the workflow produced a faster or better decision. A place to begin may be one recurring finance process with an identifiable owner, measurable cycle time and clear output. Automate part of it. Track the exceptions. Measure the review burden. Then decide whether to expand. See also: What IBM’s Quantum Breakthrough Means for the $100 Million CFO OpenAI’s model is ambitious because its technology allows it to be. The mid-market lesson is much more practical: AI-native finance does not begin when the entire department becomes automated. It begins when finance stops using people to repeatedly reconstruct information a system should already know. OpenAI’s examples of AI-native finance built atop zero day closes span forecasting, procurement, tax and investor relations. The common thread is not flashy automation. It is repetitive knowledge work with clear inputs, defined outputs and human review. That profile exists everywhere in mid-market finance. Variance commentary, audit support, covenant reporting, board materials, contract review, cash forecasting and recurring management analysis are all candidates because they consume skilled labor without necessarily requiring skilled labor at every step. The more interesting dividing line is therefore not “strategic” versus “administrative.” It is whether a workflow repeatedly forces finance employees to reconstruct information that already exists somewhere in the company. If the answer is yes, AI may have an economic case. For all PYMNTS B2B coverage, subscribe to the daily B2B Newsletter."

      "newsworthiness_score": 0.939,
      "group_id": 6887153,
      "full_text": "The fintech space is moving along at an alarming pace, and these skills for staying informed and capable should be kept in mind. In the rapidly evolving STEM space, it can sometimes feel as though the moment you have mastered a new skill or overcome an obstacle, a larger, more pressing challenge takes its place. It can be extraordinarily difficult to stay up to date on industry changes, but for ambitious jobseekers and professionals, falling behind is not an option. The fintech space is no different. Modernisation, particularly in the area of digital assets and financial infrastructure, has resulted in an ecosystem where the vast majority of the work has moved online and the skills needed to stay informed have become increasingly complex. But that doesn’t mean that it is impossible to do so – in fact, now more than ever there are a myriad of fun, innovative and convenient ways to upskill, without breaking the bank. With that in mind, as part of SiliconRepublic.com’s August fintech and financial infrastructure coverage, here are some of the skills that you should definitely keep on your radar if you envision a long and successful career in this space. Keep it real To start off, it is critical that in an evolved, automated and complex environment, where often you are dealing with deeply sensitive information, you have an arsenal of interpersonal skills. Many professionals or jobseekers in finance might assume that to get ahead, all you need are the technical skills and a decent understanding of the lingo. While this is an equally important component of a fintech or finance-based career, professionals looking for a robust skillset should ensure that they have strong communication skills, that they can address challenges and concerns with empathy, that they engage with private information ethically and that they can adapt to a rapidly changing sector. This adaptability will be evident in how you engage with new policies, tools, techniques and shifts in workplace expectations. Remember, your education, experience and qualifications are often what gets you the interview, but it is the soft skills and your attitude that indicate whether you are a good fit, not just for the industry but also the company or institution you are applying for, which will have its own set of values. Keep it relevant Hiring Now Build a future you believe in. Join our team\n\nJoin a culture that offers a world of possibilities\n\nThink for impact with Liberty IT. Delivering global software solutions\n\nInvent. Impact. Inspire. Financial literacy is the bedrock of an education or career in fintech and financial infrastructure, as it enables the student or professional to understand core concepts and make informed decisions. Financial literacy gives those who possess it an evergreen, foundational knowledge of key topics such as budgeting, investing and borrowing, and it allows individuals to better understand an evolving marketplace. Online courses teaching financial literacy are an ideal resource, as are books, videos and essays covering the subject. Fintech applications and community organisations can also give professionals or students a learning outlet that feels more ‘real’, in comparison to a book or course, as activity occurs in-person or in real-time. While the industry is always changing, many of the core concepts stay the same, so don’t get distracted by the shiny new tools to the point that you forget the basics. Keep it skilful Since the evolution of coding towards a landscape where ‘vibe-coding’ and AI-powered programming do much of the work, some might consider traditional coding skills less important now. However, this couldn’t be further from the truth. As more and more roles become cross-collaborative and skill expectations evolve, coding capabilities give finance professionals an edge in an ecosystem that thrives on niche or under-represented skills. Languages such as Python, Java and JavaScript are often used in the development of fintech platforms. Having the know-how to build an app and then deploy, maintain and use it has the potential to create an invaluable employee who is useful across multiple areas of an organisation. And it looks great on a CV or in a job interview, as it shows you are not confined by the typical skills expectations of a role. Keep it standing What is the point in having people skills, foundational skills or coding skills geared towards a fintech or finance-based career if your knowledge of the sector’s infrastructure is inherently weak? Students and professionals eager to develop a strong career in this space should ensure that they have a strong understanding of the industry’s infrastructure. That includes, but isn’t limited to, banking systems (traditional and neo-banks), payment platforms, and the risk and compliance organisations that govern it all. Areas to prioritise include financial analysis, finance modelling skills, increased awareness of regulatory and political factors, and complex project management, among others. All in all, as long as you can say you are continuously learning and can show that you aren’t allowing your skills to atrophy, a good employer, who is serious about ensuring a candidate is a good fit, will reconsider the potential you bring. Don’t miss out on the knowledge you need to succeed. Sign up for the Daily Brief, Silicon Republic’s digest of need-to-know sci-tech news."

      "newsworthiness_score": 1,
      "group_id": 7759811,
      "full_text": "Southeast Asia's fast-growing fintech sector is facing a major workforce challenge. A regional study by the Global Fintech Institute, supported by the Asian Development Bank, finds an estimated 48% technical skills gap and a 46% soft-skills gap across Indonesia, the Philippines, Singapore and Vietnam. The report shows that the issue is no longer just about finding more programmers or data experts. Governments and businesses also need people who understand finance, regulation, risk, communication and leadership.\n\nThe concern is already affecting companies. Between 84% and 88% of surveyed organizations said closing fintech skills gaps is an immediate priority, while 84% to 90% said these shortages are having a high impact on their business objectives. Vietnam reported the highest impact at 90%, followed by the Philippines at 88%, Indonesia at 86% and Singapore at 84%. The survey covered 100 middle- to senior-level professionals and related stakeholders, with 25 respondents in each country.\n\nSkills gaps are becoming an economic issue\n\nThe report identifies digital payments, cybersecurity, regulatory technology, digital lending and AI-enabled services as the most important technical areas. But it also stresses that technology skills alone are not enough.\n\nEmployers want staff who can solve problems, communicate clearly, work across departments and understand banking, financial regulation and risk. Core financial services knowledge was the top industry knowledge priority in all four countries.\n\nThis has wider economic importance. A shortage of skilled workers can slow innovation, increase compliance costs and raise operational risks. It can also reduce fintech companies' ability to expand services to unbanked and underserved communities. For policymakers, fintech workforce development is therefore linked to financial inclusion, productivity, cybersecurity and digital-economy growth.\n\nDifferent countries need different responses\n\nThe four markets face different challenges.\n\nSingapore has the strongest fintech and training ecosystem, but specialist talent is expensive and difficult to recruit, especially for smaller companies. Indonesia has strong growth in payments and digital lending, but continues to face basic digital-skills gaps and uneven access to training outside major cities.\n\nThe Philippines has a large English-speaking workforce and a strong IT-BPM industry, giving it a good base for expansion. However, it still needs more advanced skills in AI, cybersecurity and data science. Vietnam has a young and increasingly technology-oriented workforce, but access to structured fintech training remains uneven.\n\nThe report therefore argues against a single training model for the entire region. Indonesia and Vietnam may need more investment in basic skills and access, while the Philippines can build on its service-sector workforce. Singapore may need to focus more on advanced specialist skills and continuous reskilling.\n\nBetter training, not just more training\n\nOne of the strongest findings is that the problem is not simply a lack of courses. Many existing programs are seen as too generic, outdated or weakly connected to real fintech jobs.\n\nBusinesses prefer practical learning. About 57% of respondents supported cross-functional training and knowledge-sharing programs, 55% favored access to mentors and industry experts, and 49% supported incentives linked to upskilling. Public-private partnerships with universities and training providers received even stronger support, with 61% identifying them as an effective way to close skills gaps.\n\nFor international development partners, this suggests that funding should go beyond scholarships and short courses. Support could focus on industry-led curriculum design, employer-based learning, digital credentials, quality assurance, regional training platforms and stronger regulator capacity.\n\nA regional framework could reduce fragmentation\n\nThe report proposes a common ASEAN skills framework built around three areas: Technical Skills, Soft Skills and Industry Knowledge. It uses four proficiency levels—Basic, Developing, Competent and Advanced- and is designed to help employers, universities and governments use the same language when describing skills.\n\nThe aim is not to replace national systems, but to make them easier to compare across borders. The report also recommends digital micro-credentials, cross-border projects, virtual mentorship and future mutual-recognition pilots in areas such as payments, cybersecurity and RegTech.\n\nFor the private sector, this could widen access to talent and reduce hiring costs. For governments, it could improve workforce planning and policy coordination. For development partners, it creates opportunities to support regional standards, regulator training and portable credentials.\n\nThe report's central message is simple: ASEAN's fintech growth will depend not only on technology and investment, but on people. If the region can build workers who combine technical skills with financial knowledge, regulatory understanding and strong problem-solving abilities, it will be better placed to scale digital finance safely, competitively and inclusively."

Altman began discussing the idea of creating one’s business as opposed to working up the corporate ladder “just doing stuff.” Now in the age of AI, in part thanks to his own startup, it’s that much easier for someone to turn down a prestigious banking internship (Goldman’s intern class acceptance rate is now below 1%.) Working at an establishment like Goldman, Altman said, meaning being around “people [who] put too much effort into trying to get taken seriously.” Because banking careers are inherently hierarchical, there’s no room to create. Startups, on the other hand, offer that freedom. “I think this is much more true now than ever before,” he said. “You can make a whole startup, kind of by yourself in a room with a lot of AI tokens but not much else, and you don’t need anyone to take you seriously to do that.” “You can get a long way in life and career just by doing stuff,” Altman said, adding that OpenAI gives young adults plenty of responsibility for major projects as the company believes in “identifying and betting on young, unproven talent.” Recruiting from Wall Street Years after Altman turned down the offer, his company is now recruiting Wall Street talent. Both junior investment bankers and senior banking veterans—including former Morgan Stanley managing director Alisha Lehr just last month—are among OpenAI’s latest recruits. It has enlisted more than 100 former investment bankers from Goldman Sachs, JPMorgan and Morgan Stanley to train AI models to build financial models and automate work traditionally handled by junior bankers, and there could be plenty more seats to fill. OpenAI reportedly plans to grow its workforce to 8,000 by the end of the year. OpenAI itself increasingly expects finance people to be AI-native. CFO Sarah Friar said in June that she probably wouldn’t hire a finance employee today who didn’t know how to use a tool like Codex, comparing it to knowing Excel. “I would never hire a finance person who didn’t know how to use Excel, and I probably wouldn’t hire a finance person today that doesn’t know how to use a tool like Codex,” she said. As some bankers leave for jobs at AI labs, finance is also turning into a battleground for enterprise AI business more broadly. Anthropic launched roughly 10 pre-built AI agents for financial workflows, including pitchbooks, earnings analysis, credit memos and underwriting, with Claude is already in production at JPMorgan, Goldman and Citi."

### Entity monitoring : track a specific person or event across all coverage

Rather than filtering by which company published or was mentioned in an article, you can track every article that names a specific **person** or **event**, regardless of which company or industry it falls under. This is useful for monitoring an executive, founder, regulator, or ongoing situation (a conflict, policy rollout, or investigation) as it's covered across the entire news index.

Use the following filters:

- entity\_person\_list filter set to the person's name, e.g. `Elon Musk`, to track every article that names them.
- entity\_event\_list filter works the same way for a named event, e.g. `Iran war`, if you want to follow coverage of a specific event instead of a person.

<CodeGroup>

```bash cURL
curl --location 'https://api.akta.pro/api/v1/news?entity_person_list=Elon%20Musk&limit=10' \
--header 'x-api-key: YOUR API KEY'
```

```python Python
import requests

url = "https://api.akta.pro/api/v1/news?entity_person_list=Elon Musk&limit=10"

payload = {}
headers = {
  'x-api-key': 'YOUR API KEY'
}

response = requests.request("GET", url, headers=headers, data=payload)

print(response.text)
```

</CodeGroup>

      "full_text": "  \n\n### Thank you for registering\n\nPlease refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in\n\n[Jump to content](#main-content)\n\nSwipe for next article\n\n### Your support helps us to tell the story\n\nRead more\n\n[Support Now](/donate?regSourceMethod=Donations)\n\nFrom reproductive rights to climate change to Big Tech, The Independent is on the ground when the story is developing. Whether it's investigating the financials of Elon Musk's pro-Trump PAC or producing our latest documentary, 'The A Word', which shines a light on the American women fighting for reproductive rights, we know how important it is to parse out the facts from the messaging.\n\nAt such a critical moment in US history, we need reporters on the ground. Your donation allows us to keep sending journalists to speak to both sides of the story.\n\nThe Independent is trusted by Americans across the entire political spectrum. And unlike many other quality news outlets, we choose not to lock Americans out of our reporting and analysis with paywalls. We believe quality journalism should be available to everyone, paid for by those who can afford it.\n\n**Your support makes all the difference.**\n\nRead more\n\nA fisherman stepped ashore at a small harbour on Hungary’s Lake Velence last week, climbing up a dock plank left steeply inclined by record-low water levels.\n\nÁrpád Selmeci noted that steering even a small fishing vessel on Hungary's third-largest lake has grown ever more challenging, as depths have sunk to approximately 20 centimetres (8 inches). This sits drastically beneath the 150 centimetres (5 feet) considered optimal during August, leading to the closure of swimming beaches that were once major tourist attractions.\n\nThe shrinking of Lake Velence reflects a wider water emergency facing Hungary, where increasingly extended droughts and heatwaves driven by climate change have hit the European continent hard.\n\n“I've lived on Lake Velence since I was a child, and I've never seen the water so low,\" said Selmeci, 58. \"It's terrible. My heart aches every single moment.”\n\nThis summer, wildfires scorched the continent from the [United Kingdom](/topic/united-kingdom) to [Greece](/topic/greece), while in Hungary, drought has damaged crop yields, led to drinking water shortages and threatened to shut down the country's only nuclear power plant.\n\nYet the lack of rainfall linked to human-driven climate change is only part of Hungary's water problem. Experts say the country's long-standing practice of channeling water away from its landscape and draining natural wetlands has left it especially vulnerable as droughts become more frequent and severe.\n\nopen image in gallery\n\nPeople walk on sandbanks exposed by record-low water levels in the Danube River after drought and extreme heat near Nagymaros, Hungary, on Aug. 18, 2026. (AP Photo/Bela Szandelszky) (Copyright 2026 The Associated Press. All rights reserved.)\n\nAround 99% of Hungary’s lands are currently under severe or extreme drought conditions, according to the national meteorological service, and desertification, a process where vegetation recedes because of high heat and low rainfall, threatens much of the Great Hungarian Plain.\n\nYet more than a quarter of Hungary consists of low-lying land that naturally retains precipitation, and in past centuries its many rivers and streams made it rich in freshwater resources. However, a 2023 study published in the journal Nature estimated Hungary’s historical wetland loss at more than 80%.\n\nZsuzsanna Ujj, a biodiversity policy specialist and project coordinator with environmental group Friends of the Earth Hungary, said the country “has not been a good steward of its waters in the last 200 years.”\n\nA major contributor is Hungary's network of more than 40,000 kilometers (25,000 miles) of drainage canals — a length that exceeds the circumference of the Earth — which drain accumulated precipitation from fields, wetlands and the soil to make room for agriculture and other enterprises.\n\nThe priority on draining land rather than allowing natural flooding cycles to occur has contributed to a consistent drop in the water table and the drying out of wetlands and topsoil. With less water available to evaporate, the air dries out, further reducing the chances of localized precipitation.\n\nUjj said that Hungary — which is among the most drought-exposed countries in Europe and ranks 16th globally, according to a report by the Organization for Economic Cooperation and Development — “needs to realign its water management practices, shifting from a drainage-based approach to one focused on water retention.”\n\nopen image in gallery\n\nThe bed of Lake Garancsi is exposed as the popular tourist destination near Budapest after the lake died out following an extended drought period near Tinnye, Hungary, on Aug. 8, 2026. (AP Photo/Bela Szandelszky) (Copyright 2026 The Associated Press. All rights reserved.)\n\nThe drying out of Hungary isn't limited to streams and wetlands. Lake Garancsi, a small recreational lake around 20 kilometers (12 miles) northwest of Budapest, was once a popular destination for fishing, swimming and ice skating, and was the setting of the 2001 cult Hungarian comedy “Üvegtigris” (“Glass Tiger\").\n\nBut the lake, present since the Middle Ages, has shrunk so dramatically in recent years that none of the previous recreational activities are permitted, and most of the former lake bed is now exposed, cracked and dry.\n\nWhat remains of the water is so shallow and warm that all of the fish and most of the other wildlife have disappeared, and authorities have predicted no water will be left by the end of summer.\n\nZoltán Mettner, who runs the Glass Tiger bar on the shores of the lake, said conditions have gotten worse every year. After spending years beside the lake, he said looking at it now is “terrible, there’s no other way to put it.”\n\n“It’s sad enough that we’ve let things get this far, and that we couldn’t have taken gradual steps to prevent this lake from being completely destroyed,” he said.\n\nopen image in gallery\n\nPeople walk on a sandbar underneath the Danube's Margareth Bridge in downtown Budapest, Hungary, on Aug. 5, 2026, as the river, which runs through 10 European countries, has receded never-before-seen low water levels. (AP Photo/Bela Szandelszky, File) (Copyright 2026 The Associated Press. All rights reserved)\n\nHungary's new government has promised to begin taking those steps.\n\nIn late June, on the same day the country set a high temperature record of 42 Celsius (107.6 Fahrenheit), Prime Minister [Péter Magyar](/topic/peter-magyar) told lawmakers that his government would put water management policies “on new footing,” and launch a comprehensive water conservation program to mitigate the effects of drought.\n\n\"Many have said year after year that water shortage will be one of Hungary’s problems and one of the most important strategic issues to be solved. They were right,” Magyar said.\n\nIn addition to poor water retention practices, Magyar said that aging water utility infrastructure causes 20-25% of the country’s drinking water to leak out through damaged pipes, costing some 17 billion forints ($54.4 million) annually.\n\n“For years, even the existence of the problem was denied and relativized,” Magyar said. Now, he added, “water retention will be the basic principle of Hungarian water management.”\n\nopen image in gallery\n\nBoats lie on cracked soil at a dried-out marina on Lake Velence, Hungary, on Aug. 13. 2026. (AP Photo/Bela Szandelszky) (Copyright 2026 The Associated Press. All rights reserved.)\n\nYet implementing such sweeping changes will be time consuming and costly, while Hungary's current water crisis threatens its ability to produce adequate energy.\n\nThe Danube, the European Union's longest river which runs through 10 countries from southwestern Germany to the Black Sea, has set successive records this summer for low water levels, threatening to shut down Hungary's only nuclear power plant, which uses water from the river to cool its reactors, for the first time in its 44-year history.\n\nHungary's government has taken emergency interventions to raise the water level at the Paks plant, including sinking two 80-meter (262-foot) barges just upriver and beginning to install a riverbed sill designed to divert more water toward the plant's pumps.\n\nIn such drought conditions, wetlands near rivers like the Danube normally act as a “natural reservoir” for retaining water when weather gets dry, said Ujj, the biodiversity specialist.\n\nThe extremely low level of the Danube — as well as the increased chances for more frequent and intense floods — are effects of human-driven climate change that can be partially mitigated through appropriate land use and restoration of wetlands and other natural habitats, she added.\n\n“We do indeed expect water levels to fluctuate more, and we also expect precipitation patterns to become more extreme with drier summers,” she said. \"However, restoring wetlands and increasing evaporation in certain areas could help boost precipitation during the summer months.”\n\n### More about\n\n[Hungary](/topic/hungary)[Péter Magyar](/topic/peter-magyar)[Danube river](/topic/danube-river)[United       "title": "Galaxy General Wang He: Promoting Core Breakthrough Moments in Embodied Intelligence and Humanoid Robots",

      "ai_summary": "Wang He, founder and CTO of Galaxy General Robotics, presented the company's roadmap for embodied intelligence at the 2026 World Robotics Conference, highlighting breakthroughs in humanoid robot autonomy and dexterous manipulation. The company showcased its 'Galaxy Star Brain' framework and demonstrated capabilities such as autonomous tennis playing and complex hand operations using reinforcement learning. ",

      "full_text": "On August 20, Wang He, founder and chief technology officer of Galaxy General Robotics Co., Ltd., delivered a keynote speech titled \"Driving Core Breakthrough Moments in Embodied Intelligence and Humanoid Robots\" at the main forum of the 2026 World Robotics Conference - \"Chapter 2: Application Traction.\" Below is an excerpt of the speech, organized by the investment community (ID: pedaily2012): Wang He: I am very honored to share with you today at the WRC main forum our progress and determination in driving core breakthrough moments in embodied intelligence and humanoid robots. Every person sitting here, whether a guest or an audience member, is envisioning the moment when humanoid robots enter various industries and households. The most critical aspect here is to use models to drive humanoid robots towards generality, which inevitably involves whether the robot's body is flexible and free enough. More importantly, we need to have a humanoid robot brain that can control the body to perform various actions; the small brain and big brain are the soul of humanoid robots and the most valuable part of the embodied intelligence industry. Therefore, today we want to discuss how Galaxy General views the roadmap to generality. The first milestone is to prove the value of humanoid bodies in one endeavor, fully utilizing the value of humanoid bodies. In March of this year, we released the world's first fully autonomous humanoid robot playing tennis. We can see that in this work, the humanoid robot can maintain balance with its whole body and precisely control its wrist, allowing it to hit the ball, with the ball landing in the target area of the racket, and the quality of the return shot being high. On top of these controls, we need to achieve strategic intent in adjusting the ball, defeating human opponents through forward and backward adjustments and left and right adjustments. The lower level is full-body control, while the higher level is the ability for strategic games, truly showcasing the capabilities of humanoid robots. Upon our release, it sparked global discussions online, with Elon Musk immediately stating that this work is inference. Later, an independent researcher mentioned that such work confirms the arrival of the AlphaGo moment for sports, to which Elon Musk immediately commented, \"Yes.\" When we see the humanoid robot running around the tennis court, moving autonomously, and demonstrating its control capabilities, it is akin to how DeepMind defined Go in 2016; we have released the autonomous capabilities of humanoid robots, which is inseparable from Galaxy General's long-term accumulation of simulation and reinforcement learning instances. From basic return learning to the second stage of self-adversarial learning, we have accumulated over 10,000 years of training time in the simulator for tennis, which is even more challenging than Go, because Go is a game in the digital world and physical world, while we have to transfer our training from the simulator to the real world. This is also a unique world-leading technology of Galaxy General. In a few days, at the opening ceremony of the World Humanoid Robot Conference, we will unveil a truly new generation of tennis playing technology, showcasing greater human-like capabilities, including human-robot mixed doubles, which will also be broadcast live on CCTV, featuring Olympic champions and popular stars. We welcome everyone to witness the global debut of the true AlphaGo moment for humanoid robots. Many people say that your tennis racket is just being held. This spring, we showcased the dexterous hand brain area of our self-developed Galaxy Star Brain model, demonstrating the ability to crack a walnut. This walnut has varying degrees of tightness, and its characteristics are constantly changing; it cannot be achieved with a fixed trajectory, otherwise, it will either crush the walnut or drop it. This also reflects the further training of model capabilities through reinforcement learning in the real world. We can compare this with some of the top international humanoid robot teams. On the left, Figure shows a dexterous hand performing the action of opening a bottle cap; it grips and turns loosely, which is not continuous. I cannot speak for their technical principles, but the left operation can be achieved through remote control data collection, while the right side's continuous coordination cannot be collected through remote control; we use reinforcement learning on the right, learning directly in the simulator, achieving efficient same trial. Today, we see that the higher the difficulty of fine hand operations, the less feasible it is to use remote control events, and the more it requires same trial, which is a more advanced technology route that can showcase the full capabilities of the dexterous hand. The left side shows the pen spinning released by NVIDIA in 2023, which is operated through reinforcement learning in the simulator. Unfortunately, due to frequent collisions between the pen and fingers during the spinning process, it makes it difficult for the simulator to achieve complete fidelity. On the right is the dexterous hand pen spinning achieved for the first time globally by Galaxy General, which can now perform high-dynamic operations, achieving a level of skill higher than that of flying in the air and landing back on the fingertip. None of these operations can be achieved through remote control; they must be realized through reinforcement learning and same trial technology. The dexterous hand operation is the crown jewel, and Galaxy General once again showcases its capability in application milestones. The next important step is how to transition from specialized models to general ones. In the digital world, we see that GPT has defined the paradigm. What is embodied GPT? How can a large embodied model absorb various tasks and data to move towards generality? I believe this is the most concerning question after everyone has seen our breakthroughs in various high-difficulty tasks. This brings us to the framework of Galaxy Star Brain. Humanoid robots need both high-level action intent generation and rough trajectory generation, as well as full-body control and high-frequency feedback. Therefore, we have built a brain and a small brain for humanoid robots, connected by a bridge, communicating between the slower big brain and the faster small brain. This entire bionic framework is the foundational large model of Galaxy Star Brain aimed at general humanoid robots. The big brain and small brain here are both large models, each seeking its own GPT paradigm. What is the GPT paradigm of the big brain? Google proposed VLA, and OpenAI proposed the world model; both models have their strengths and weaknesses. VLA can absorb a large amount of action data, but it can only absorb data with action labels. The world model can absorb a large amount of video data, but it still does not learn how much each joint of the robot should rotate. Therefore, truly integrating VLA and the world model can combine action-labeled data from robots with vast amounts of unlabeled scene data from humans to learn human operational experiences. This paradigm is the world action model. This year, NVIDIA's director of embodied intelligence mentioned in an interview with Sequoia that ONE is the lifelong game of robots. The endgame technology for robots was first proposed globally by Galaxy General. Today, if you search for the world action model on our website, the first article globally is from Galaxy General, published in March 2025 and accepted by ICCV 2025. This paradigm is now leading the development of large models and brains in embodied intelligence. Just yesterday, I searched, and there are already 171 articles on the world action model; when we published it in 2025, there was only one article, which was ours, and the second article came out in December after a long time. Today, with Galaxy Star Brain's ON, we propose modeling in latent space for the future. We have truly achieved cross-entity, cross-task, and cross-scene operations, where one model can serve as a transport robot for unskilled hands. Behind such a model is the integration of simulation real-world data and human data into a new, unified data paradigm for large embodied models, which also verifies that as long as we use human data without any labels, we can continue to see the robot's action prediction loss function further decrease. These achievements couple with world-leading true machine reinforcement learning technology. At our booth, everyone can personally experience the robot making breakfast, whether it's pulling away the bread or covering the bottle to prevent it from pouring; the robot can handle everything autonomously. We are about to release technology that will showcase the first ten-minute breakfast, including the full process of preparing fruits, salads, and various ingredients, ultimately making a sandwich. Such capabilities not only reflect our machine model capabilities but also demonstrate the powerful training capabilities of real-world reinforcement learning. As mentioned earlier, what does the small brain need? The small brain needs to control the body to perform various actions. Today, Galaxy General's full-body control brain area is a truly general small brain. This general small brain model is also a large model with hundreds of millions of parameters. Under the control of this large model, we can remotely control humanoid robots to perform various tasks, including taking care of cats at home, cleaning cat litter, providing emergency support in various scenarios, and performing high-dynamic actions, all without the need for pre-recorded trajectories, allowing for real-time control. This is the small brain; whatever intent the big brain issues, the small brain executes the actions smoothly and resiliently. Such a small brain used to be a specialized small brain that overfitted to a single trajectory, but today Galaxy General has built a general small brain. Under this general small brain, we can see a comparison with NVIDIA's top general small brain, where our latency is lower, actions are more precise, and we have better stability in balancing high-difficulty actions. We have verified that under 100,000 hours of human data, the loss curve of the general small brain continues to decrease, which is the Scaling Low of the general small brain. Under the general small brain, we released our own small humanoid robot, Galaxy Star·Galbot ET1, at the sub-forum yesterday. We can achieve real-time dancing with humans, extracting actions in real-time and showcasing high-difficulty dance moves using the general small brain. The intelligent agent can recognize and extract human dance movements in real-time, including ground actions and handstands. Once you have a truly general small brain, you can unlock more real-time generated spontaneous actions and support further interaction between robots and human intelligence. This is the native intelligent agent of the physical world. We have begun to conduct dance drainage, where the entire intelligent agent generates dance drainage, leading people to interact and sell. After the general small and big brains, I believe everyone is most concerned about when embodied intelligence will usher in the ChatGPT moment. This question leads us to consider what the ChatGPT of embodied intelligence is. I define the ChatGPT of embodied intelligence as the ability to achieve a success rate of 70%-80% on tasks that humans do not need special learning for. A success rate of 70%-80% is still not enough; our next step is to figure out how to make such machine models easily retrainable, allowing individuals without any professional skills to retrain the models to achieve a high success rate of 100% in their specific scenarios. First, how to achieve a 70-80% success rate on a wide range of tasks is the machine model. Today, under the paradigm of the general small brain, the most important thing is to further expand the data and increase computational power, achieving Scaling UP. This is the Galaxy Star Tree, a five-level pyramid of embodied intelligence, from internet data, human data, synthetic data to real machine remote control data and real machine feedback data, each layer plays its own role. Human data has been built since 2022 and remains the largest labeled dataset of human hand operations to date. In 2022, we were the first to have people wear helmets to collect dual-hand operations. Today, we have also released the Astra Set, our own head-mounted and dual-hand UMI data collection device, which can achieve high-precision UMI data collection, greatly promoting the progress of machine models. With this hardware support, we now have thousands of head-mounted and dual-hand UAI collection sources collecting data across various industries. Behind this is also our complete online data management, labeling, and filtering system. With such comprehensive data construction, we now have 500,000 hours of high-precision UMI data and 500,000 hours of human data without any handheld mechanical devices, totaling 1 million hours of data that allows robots to learn human actions fully. The robot's actions primarily come from synthetic data, which is also the entire set of simulation assets, action generation, simulation verification, and final same trial pipeline built by Galaxy General. This pipeline has led the world in several areas, including the most challenging simulations. Today, we can simulate flexible objects and dexterous hands, even achieving the most difficult shuffling simulation, including showcasing the pen spinning, with the final same trial technology being behind the leading simulator, which has enabled the first breakthrough globally. On top of the simulation data, we also need a portion of real machine remote control data, which is relatively expensive. However, today Galaxy General has established training grounds in six provinces and cities, continuously collecting application data from various industries, ultimately deploying robots across various sectors, including 100 smart pharmacies in 45 cities and 200 locations in the Galaxy Space Warehouse, and accumulating 80,000 hours of real-world data from companies like CATL and various factories and medical care facilities. This data allows robots to further understand what they do well and what they do poorly, enabling continuous growth. Under this Galaxy Star Tree, we judge that the entire industry's data is thriving. From Galaxy's perspective, we are about to rapidly reach the ChatGPT level of machine models. The only condition is whether we can quickly implement such machine models. Therefore, we have also developed the Galaxy Star Brain WAM—TTT technology, allowing customers to simply shoot videos without needing to collect any remote control data to retrain the model. This way, users without any educational background can collect data; as long as they wear a camera, they can capture videos of their work. Our retraining only requires video loss without any labels or actions to complete the retraining, making the speed of model implementation in real-world scenarios very fast, and the threshold is very low, allowing various industries to utilize machine models. Today, we assess when the ChatGPT moment of embodied intelligence will arrive. With the accumulation of data today and the continuous breakthroughs in retraining technology, the entire industry of Galaxy General will reach the ChatGPT of embodied intelligence by 2028. Once we reach the ChatGPT of embodied intelligence, the scale and speed of implementation across the industry will significantly increase. By the end of the 14th Five-Year Plan, we will see Galaxy General deploying hundreds of thousands of humanoid robots across various industries, which will truly lead us towards multi-scenario applications and the future of diverse applications of wheeled bipedal dexterous hands. Our current construction of an application ecosystem across various industries is just the first step; in the future, we will further open up the secondary development ecosystem, using our machine models, hardware, and data collection devices, along with a complete data management and retraining system, to help more enterprises establish their own embodied intelligence teams, allowing them to develop the skills they want in their own scenarios and influence the next generation. Through top-level events like the International Olympic AI Competition, we can cultivate the next generation of youth in embodied intelligence, enabling the entire industry to eagerly anticipate the arrival of the AGI moment in embodied intelligence, truly achieving the quantity of mobile phones, the price of cars, and the benefits of large models, opening up a trillion-dollar market and welcoming the arrival of the Fourth Industrial Revolution. My report ends here. Thank you all!"

      "full_text": "Satellite connectivity could offer another route to internet access without depending on terrestrial infrastructure. Musk’s remarks also draw attention to the uneven distribution of internet access between rural and urban India.\n\nHis post comes as Starlink’s efforts to secure regulatory clearance and enter the Indian market gather fresh attention.\n\nStarlink would help the least served in India, especially in rural areas https://t.co/ffCvpL61WQ — Elon Musk (@elonmusk) August 21, 2026\n\nMusk highlights India’s rural connectivity gap\n\nMusk made the recent remark on X while responding to a post from the account DogeDesigner, which argued that India needs Starlink. Musk wrote, “Starlink would help the least served in India, especially in rural areas.”\n\nMust Read: X reveals what makes posts go viral, and likes are not the biggest factor\n\nAdvertisement\n\nThe post highlighted that 11,256 of India’s listed villages had no 4G coverage as of May 2026. It also pointed to the gap in internet subscription density between rural and urban areas.\n\nFor users in remote areas, Musk’s pitch is centred on Starlink’s ability to provide connectivity without relying on conventional terrestrial infrastructure. Satellite broadband could potentially reach locations where expanding mobile or fibre networks is more difficult.\n\nStarlink’s India plans face regulatory hurdles\n\nMusk’s latest post comes amid renewed efforts by Starlink to enter India. Starlink has reapplied to the Indian National Space Promotion and Authorisation Centre (IN-SPACe) for approval of its Gen 2 satellite constellation, which includes newer capabilities such as direct-to-device connectivity. The company’s earlier approval process had faced a freeze over security concerns.\n\nAdvertisement\n\nThe renewed application means Starlink’s India ambitions are back in focus, while regulatory clearances and spectrum-related decisions remain important before the service can begin commercial operations."

Inc.](https://www.bloomberg.com/quote/60384Z:US)’s satellite-based internet service as a solution for India’s most underserved rural regions, even though the operator’s launch remains stalled in the country over security concerns.\n\n“Starlink would help the least served in India, especially in rural areas,” he said in a [post](https://x.com/elonmusk/status/2090616381599629749 \"link\") on X early Monday, replying to another user who highlighted the country’s patchy internet connectivity. Centre denies Revanth Reddy clearance for US visit, cites ‘political angle’\n \n\nTrending Topics\n\n* [PM Modi](https://www.moneycontrol.com/news/india/pm-modi-asks-officials-to-break-silos-engage-more-with-youth-at-high-level-meeting-14011932.html \"PM Modi\")\n* [Chidambaram](https://www.moneycontrol.com/news/india/chidambaram-backs-vijay-govt-s-stand-on-delimitation-current-numbers-should-be-extended-for-25-years-14012052.html \"Chidambaram \")\n* [DMK](https://www.moneycontrol.com/news/india/dmk-targets-vijay-says-tamil-nadu-cm-stayed-silent-dks-spoke-up-on-mekedatu-cauvery-issue-14011966.html \"DMK \")\n* [Sajjan Kumar](https://www.moneycontrol.com/news/india/bjp-targets-congress-over-sajjan-kumar-tributes-eulogising-killers-14012118.html \"Sajjan Kumar\")\n* [Elon Musk](https://www.moneycontrol.com/news/india/elon-musk-pitches-starlink-for-india-s-rural-internet-gap-amid-fresh-licence-bid-14012059.html \"Elon Musk\")\n\n# Centre denies Revanth Reddy clearance for US visit, cites ‘political angle’\n\nFollowing the denial of permission, Reddy directed the official 'Telangana Rising' delegation to continue with its planned US visit\n\n[Moneycontrol News](https://www.moneycontrol.com/author/moneycontrol-news-7285/ \"Moneycontrol News\")\n\nAugust 21, 2026 / 11:47 IST\n\nTelangana CM Revanth Reddy\n\nTelangana Chief Minister A Revanth Reddy has been denied permission by the Centre to visit the United States during his ongoing 10-day tour of the UK and US, according to Indian Express.\n\n[\n](https://media-moneycontrol.akamaized.net/1234564/manifest.m3u8)\n\nReddy, accompanied by a delegation of state officials, began the tour on August 20 with plans to meet business leaders and sign memoranda of understanding aimed at attracting investments to Telangana. However, the Chief Minister’s Office said on Friday that the Ministry of External Affairs had denied political clearance for his US visit.\n\nAccording to IE,  officials in the Telangana Chief Minister’s Office are seeking further clarity from the Ministry of External Affairs on the reasons for denying political clearance. However, they said Chief Minister A [Revanth Reddy](https://www.moneycontrol.com/topic/revanth-reddy/) will return to Hyderabad from London on August 23 instead of proceeding to Boston.\n\nFollowing the denial of permission, Reddy directed the official “Telangana Rising” delegation to continue with its planned US visit. The delegation is expected to sign MoUs with leading educational institutions, including Harvard University, the Massachusetts Institute of Technology (MIT), Northeastern University and Virginia Tech.\n\nReddy kicked off a 10-day tour of the UK and US after arriving in London on Thursday. In the UK, he is scheduled to visit the Blavatnik School of Government at the University of Oxford and the Emirates Stadium for an EPL football presentation, according to the state government.\n\nReddy was scheduled to travel to the US next week as part of his 10-day UK-US tour. His itinerary included visits to the campuses of Harvard University and the Massachusetts Institute of Technology (MIT).\n\nReddy was also scheduled to interact with officials from Virginia Tech University in Boston on August 25. His US itinerary included official meetings as well as a meet-and-greet event with the Indian diaspora in New York, organised by the US chapter of the Indian Overseas Congress (IOC).\n\nBefore beginning his foreign tour, Reddy was in New Delhi on Wednesday to attend the Congress Working Committee (CWC) meeting.\n\nRead More\n\nMoneycontrol News\n\nfirst published: Aug 21, 2026 11:23 am\n\nDiscover the latest [Business News](https://www.moneycontrol.com/news/business/ \"Business News\"), [Sensex](https://www.moneycontrol.com/sensex/bse/sensex-live \"Sensex\"), and [Nifty](https://www.moneycontrol.com/indian-indices/nifty-50-9.html \"Nifty\") updates.
